=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
In the nation of AtCoder, there are N cities numbered 1 to N, and M roads numbered 1 to M.
Road i connects cities A_i and B_i bidirectionally and has a length of C_i.
For each i = 1, \ldots, M, determine whether the following two values are different.

- The shortest distance from city 1 to city N when all roads are passable
- The shortest distance from city 1 to city N when the M - 1 roads other than road i are passable

If city N can be reached from city 1 in one of these cases but not the other, the two values are considered different.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M
A_1 B_1 C_1
\vdots
A_M B_M C_M

Output

Print M lines. The i-th line should contain Yes if the shortest distance from city 1 to city N when all roads are passable is different from the shortest distance when the M - 1 roads other than road i are passable, and No otherwise.
If city N can be reached from city 1 in one of these cases but not the other, the two values are considered different.

Constraints


- 2 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq M \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq A_i < B_i \leq N
- All pairs (A_i, B_i) are distinct.
- 1 \leq C_i \leq 10^9
- City N can be reached from city 1 when all roads are passable.
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3 3
1 2 5
1 3 10
2 3 6

Sample Output 1

No
Yes
No

When all roads are passable, the shortest distance from city 1 to city 3 is 10.

- When the two roads other than road 1 are passable, the shortest distance is 10.
- When the two roads other than road 2 are passable, the shortest distance is 11.
- When the two roads other than road 3 are passable, the shortest distance is 10.

Sample Input 2

4 6
2 3 1
2 4 1
3 4 1
1 2 1
1 3 1
1 4 1

Sample Output 2

No
No
No
No
No
Yes

When all roads are passable, the shortest distance from city 1 to city 4 is 1.
When the five roads other than road 6 are passable, the shortest distance is 2.

Sample Input 3

2 1
1 2 1

Sample Output 3

Yes

When the zero roads other than road 1 are passable, city 2 cannot be reached from city 1.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's derive dominator tree for DAG with LCA.

Given DAG with N up to 2e5, M up to 2e5. Topological order by dist_s (strictly increasing along edges). We can process vertices in increasing dist_s. For each vertex v (except source), idom(v) = LCA of all predecessors p of v in the dominator tree. Is this always true in a DAG? Let's verify.

Dominators of v = {v} ∪ (intersection over predecessors p of Dom(p)). In a rooted tree where Dom(p) is path from root s to p in dominator tree, the intersection of paths to a set of nodes is the path to their LCA (lowest common ancestor) in the tree. Thus idom(v) is that LCA. Since all predecessors processed before v (topological), their idom known. So yes.

Need implement dynamic LCA where tree grows by adding nodes in topological order. We can use binary lifting arrays up[node][k], depth. When adding v with parent par=idom(v), set up[v][0]=par, depth[v]=depth[par]+1, compute up[v][k]. To compute LCA of a set of predecessors, we can iteratively take lca(current, pred). But number of predecessors total M, so O(M log N). Need initialize lca = first predecessor. For source s, no idom. For vertices with no predecessors? In our DAG all reachable except s? Actually all relevant vertices reachable from s, so each has at least one predecessor. Source has none.

But wait topological order by dist_s: There may be multiple vertices with same dist_s? Edges have positive weights, so if u->v, dist_s[v] = dist_s[u]+w > dist_s[u]. Thus strictly increasing. Then D = dist_s[N].

Build directed edges in DAG:

- Check if dist_s[u] + w + dist_t[v] == D. If true, add directed edge u -> v.
- Check if dist_s[v] + w + dist_t[u] == D. If true, add directed edge v -> u.

Implementation details:
- We need map from original vertex index to DAG node? We can use original 1..N. Only relevant vertices. For unreachable? All relevant reachable. For idom arrays size N+1, default 0. For source, parent[1]=0, depth[1]=0, up[1][0]=0.
- For each relevant vertex v in sorted by dist_s:
   if v == source: continue.
   lca = 0
   for each incoming edge p->v in DAG:
       if lca == 0: lca = p
       else: lca = lca(lca, p)
   parent[v] = lca
   depth[v] = depth[lca]+1
   up[v][0]=lca; for k: up[v][k]=up[ up[v][k-1] ][k-1]
Need ensure lca != 0. Source has no incoming. All other relevant vertices reachable, so yes. But what if a relevant vertex has no incoming in DAG because only source? If v != source and reachable, there is at least one path from source; first edge gives predecessor. So incoming exists.

Need lca function:
def lca(a,b):
   if depth[a] < depth[b]: a,b = b,a
   diff = depth[a]-depth[b]
   bit=0
   while diff:
      if diff&1: a = up[bit][a]
      diff >>= 1; bit += 1
   if a==b: return a
   for k in range(LOG-1,-1,-1):
      if up[k][a] != up[k][b]:
          a = up[k][a]; b = up[k][b]
   return up[0][a]
This is standard. Need ensure up[k][0]=0, depth[0]=-? We can set depth[0] = -1 maybe. For lca with 0? We never call lca with 0 except if lca_acc=0? We'll handle first predecessor. Dijkstra:
adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]
for i in range(1,M+1):
   a,b,c = ...
   A[i]=a; B[i]=b; C[i]=c
   adj[a].append((b,c))
   adj[b].append((a,c))

def dijkstra(s):
   dist=[INF]*(N+1)
   dist[s]=0
   pq=[(0,s)]
   while pq:
      d,v=heappop(pq)
      if d!=dist[v]: continue
      for to,w in adj[v]:
         nd=d+w
         if nd<dist[to]:
            dist[to]=nd; heappush(pq,(nd,to))
   return dist

Now, building DAG:

   a=A[i]; b=B[i]; w=C[i]
   if dist1[a] + w + distN[b] == D:
       dag_head[i] = b
       indeg[b] += 1
       preds[b].append(a)
   if dist1[b] + w + distN[a] == D:
       dag_head[i] = a
       indeg[a] += 1
       preds[a].append(b)

Now, dominator tree computation:
LOG = (N+1).bit_length()
up = [[0]*(N+1) for _ in range(LOG)]
depth = [0]*(N+1)
parent = [0]*(N+1)

def lca(a,b):
    if depth[a] < depth[b]:
        a,b = b,a
    diff = depth[a] - depth[b]
    k = 0
    while diff:
        if diff & 1:
            a = up[k][a]
        diff >>= 1
        k += 1
    if a == b:
        return a
    for k in range(LOG-1, -1, -1):
        if up[k][a] != up[k][b]:
            a = up[k][a]
            b = up[k][b]
    return up[0][a]

Process:
for v in sorted(relevant, key=lambda x: dist1[x]):
    if v == 1:
        continue
    acc = 0
    for p in preds[v]:
        if acc == 0:
            acc = p
        else:
            acc = lca(acc, p)
    # acc should not be 0
    parent[v] = acc
    depth[v] = depth[acc] + 1
    up[0][v] = acc
    for k in range(1, LOG):
        up[k][v] = up[k-1][ up[k-1][v] ]

Now, potential issue: We are computing dominator tree for the DAG of shortest paths, but there might be vertices with dist_s+dist_t==D that are not reachable from source in the DAG due to not including all edges? We argued no. Let's verify with an example: dist_s[v]+dist_t[v]=D. Take a shortest path from s to v. Is every edge on it in DAG? Let edge x->y on that path. Actually the s-v shortest path plus v-t shortest path is an s-t path of length D, so it is a shortest s-t path. For any vertex y on the s-v prefix, the suffix from y to t along this concatenated path has length dist_s[v]-dist_s[y] + dist_t[v] = D - dist_s[y] (because total D). Thus dist_t[y] <= D - dist_s[y], so dist_s[y]+dist_t[y] <= D; but >=D, so equality. Thus edge x->y satisfies dist_s[x]+w+dist_t[y] = dist_s[y]+dist_t[y]=D. So included. Good.

Now, let's revisit the theorem "idom(v) = LCA of predecessors in dominator tree" for DAG. Need be absolutely sure. Let's prove:

The immediate dominator is the deepest node in Dom(v) excluding v, i.e., LCA of predecessors. This is standard for DAG if all predecessors processed. Good.

A path to p that uses e goes s...u->v...p. Since p has edge p->v, then v...p->v is a cycle (v to p then back to v), impossible in DAG. Thus path to p avoids e. So e not mandatory. Therefore indeg[v] must be 1. Good.

Since p has edge to v, there is a path s->...->v->...->p->v, which contains a cycle from v to v (v->...->p->v). In a DAG, no cycles. Contradiction. Thus p reachable avoiding e. Good.

Let's prove at most one orientation with w>0:
Assume dist_s[a]+w+dist_t[b]=D and dist_s[b]+w+dist_t[a]=D.

=== ANSWER ===
to ensure it solves the problem.</think>```python
import sys
import heapq

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N, M = map(int, input().split())
    A = [0] * (M + 1)
    B = [0] * (M + 1)
    C = [0] * (M + 1)
    adj = [[] for _ in range(N + 1)]

    for i in range(1, M + 1):
        a, b, c = map(int, input().split())
        A[i] = a
        B[i] = b
        C[i] = c
        adj[a].append((b, c))
        adj[b].append((a, c))

    INF = 10**30

    def dijkstra(s):
        dist = [INF] * (N + 1)
        dist[s] = 0
        pq = [(0, s)]
        while pq:
            d, v = heapq.heappop(pq)
            if d != dist[v]:
                continue
            for to, w in adj[v]:
                nd = d + w
                if nd < dist[to]:
                    dist[to] = nd
                    heapq.heappush(pq, (nd, to))
        return dist

    dist1 = dijkstra(1)
    distN = dijkstra(N)
    D = dist1[N]

    # Build the DAG consisting only of edges that lie on some shortest path.
    indeg = [0] * (N + 1)
    preds = [[] for _ in range(N + 1)]
    dag_head = [0] * (M + 1)  # for each original road, its head in the DAG

    for i in range(1, M + 1):
        a, b, w = A[i], B[i], C[i]

        if dist1[a] + w + distN[b] == D:
            dag_head[i] = b
            indeg[b] += 1
            preds[b].append(a)

        if dist1[b] + w + distN[a] == D:
            dag_head[i] = a
            indeg[a] += 1
            preds[a].append(b)

    relevant = [v for v in range(1, N + 1) if dist1[v] + distN[v] == D]
    relevant.sort(key=lambda x: dist1[x])

    LOG = (N + 1).bit_length() + 1
    up = [[0] * (N + 1) for _ in range(LOG)]
    depth = [0] * (N + 1)
    parent = [0] * (N + 1)

    def lca(a, b):
        if depth[a] < depth[b]:
            a, b = b, a

        diff = depth[a] - depth[b]
        k = 0
        while diff:
            if diff & 1:
                a = up[k][a]
            diff >>= 1
            k += 1

        if a == b:
            return a

        for k in range(LOG - 1, -1, -1):
            if up[k][a] != up[k][b]:
                a = up[k][a]
                b = up[k][b]

        return up[0][a]

    # Dominator tree of the shortest-path DAG.
    # idom(v) = LCA of all predecessors of v.
    for v in relevant:
        if v == 1:
            continue

        acc = 0
        for p in preds[v]:
            if acc == 0:
                acc = p
            else:
                acc = lca(acc, p)

        parent[v] = acc
        depth[v] = depth[acc] + 1
        up[0][v] = acc
        for k in range(1, LOG):
            up[k][v] = up[k - 1][up[k - 1][v]]

    # Mark vertices that dominate city N.
    dominates_N = [False] * (N + 1)
    cur = N
    while cur:
        dominates_N[cur] = True
        cur = parent[cur]

    ans = []
    for i in range(1, M + 1):
        v = dag_head[i]
        if v != 0 and indeg[v] == 1 and dominates_N[v]:
            ans.append("Yes")
        else:
            ans.append("No")

    print("\n".join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
